The notable yields: Table 15.1 settles the UKMF tangle at print grade (29,000 men / 45 tanks / 52 planes full, Esbjerg in 11 days; the reduced package is 10,000 / 15 tanks — the scrape's "15,000" was a different-era variant, not this table); Fig 9.3 confirms the whole ladder and adds one detail worth keeping — a *dashed direct SACEUR→COMBALTAP line* bypassing CINCNORTH; and Fig 18.4's caption assigns the Fehmarn and Grønsund fields to German layers, with the ferry-route clearances intact. Only two Bogason asks remain, both text checks at your leisure: HOLD FAST 1960's exact wording, and an index scan for any wartime-HQ mention.

**Ch. 9/15 decompression, per your ruling.** The call stays one voice, but ch. 9 now establishes the two Danes behind the stall — the theater commander "whom the customers met only as its consequences," the air commander whose voice the corps knows — and the war's biggest allocation now carries a real pause: the voice goes off the microphone and the net holds "a division being walked somewhere for a signature." In ch. 15's bunker both men are present and distinct: the airman runs the liturgy; the theater commander, a Danish lieutenant general, sits apart and keeps himself for the rates of exchange (so the convert-it-for-me exchange now belongs unambiguously to the right office); and the maritime desk is embodied — a German Kapitän zur See down from Kiel-Holtenau, the navies' seat at a Danish table, straight off Fig 9.3.

**Finds worked** — three in-scene touches: ch. 2 gains the alliance's graduated measures catalog ("every numbered measure a signal the moment it was invoked, with the enemy as the catalog's first reader — the quiet list ran underneath"), and the assumptions ledger gains item six — the days-of-supply accountancy and the SACEUR clearing office, closed by Hvidt's "Write: two owners again. This time the shells." Ch. 6 gains the DALGAS sharpener inside Aakjær's receipts: "every autumn our own umpires burn that line in the first week, and he has not" — which makes red's restraint a legible deviation, not an oversight. Two finds deliberately not worked: the WINTEX-81 friction palette (it would cut against the chosen over-compliance mobilization design) and the Alford 1986 verdict (post-setting; apparatus-tier only).

**The line pass.** The two grep-able families turned out to already be at budget: like-a-man stands at 3 (Zawadzki's psalms, the narrows breathing through a wound, Merete's spoken line — all keeps), and the superlatives' worst went in the earlier tranche. The negation shapes in 15–16 are the census's thesis keepers (the one-customer discovery; "the lift did not fail" is fact, not definition) — no cuts. The real work was the *particular/specific* formula: four instances kept their content and lost only the formula word ("the lightness of a staff whose biggest argument has just been accepted somewhere else" survives intact minus its "particular") — which takes the family from 8 to 3 strong keeps (forty headquarters, the sickbed, the meter-payoff stillness) and, more importantly, kills the *recurrence* that made it legible as a template.

**The exit-line schedule.** Three endings flattened, each chosen because the button was also doing something else wrong:

- **Ch. 14** now ends "He left. The sheet stayed empty." — "and governed the building" was a twin of ch. 8's "began, quietly, to govern everything," so this kills a formula repeat and the emptiness speaks for itself.
- **Ch. 18** now ends "Nobody slept, and the order said nothing about sleeping." — the trailing because-clause was double delivery of Hvidt's pinned sentence forty lines up.
- **Ch. 15's beech-wood section** ends on Rylski entering the shortfall in his own hand — the "did not need to be told... kept twice" clause was the narrator elbowing the reader about the mirror.

Plus two adjacent twins made plain: the Beichtwand loses "and the name stuck" (ch. 9's market-naming keeps the sole naming button), and Aakjær's answer loses "quoted afterward in two languages" — "The visiting officer wrote that down" carries the fame concretely.

With ch. 11, ch. 12, 19a, ch. 7's new card ending, and ch. 14's earlier hands-knew-it-lived, the schedule now breaks about seven times across 22 chapters — enough irregularity that a reader's attention can't set its watch by the scene-ends. The deliberate keeps are on the record in tic-inventory, including "Walls do not sign orders" (the mannerism's strongest instance, and a plot hinge) and the promotion-epigram twins (two mentors, two promotion theories — characterful, defensible to any reviewer).

Both third-draft agenda items are closed. The next blind panel — PDF with maps and 19b, ingestion pinned, reader-ledger pricing questions, plates instrument-gated — now reads text that's been through everything we had queued for it.

Probably would be an elective with some non-majoring students, and some majors with an interest in theory or creative writing, rather than in military writing specifically.

## ASSISTANT

My position: **switch to Frimodighed.** Four grounds, one counter-brief, and I'd stand by the switch.

**1. It's the exact word, and Ærlighed isn't.** The book's rule was never about not-lying — it's about speech up the chain at personal cost: "The first officer who tells me what he believes I would prefer to hear will be told what I actually prefer, once." That is parrhesia — bold speech before authority — and *frimodighed* is precisely the Danish word for it, while *ærlighed* is honesty-as-virtue, the first word a dictionary offers for "candor." Which is in fact what it was: our "Ærlighed" is the fingerprint of a mechanical fix (the "Kandor"-isn't-Danish correction), not a choice. Hvidt paid for bold speech all war — the fights with the capitals, the F-series with his staff's initials, the refusal to time the final report to a communiqué. "Betalt fuldt ud" needs the noun that was actually bought.

**2. The sound argument.** The English motto alliterates: *candor* before *comfort*. "Frimodighed før trøst" reproduces that — f, f — where "Ærlighed før trøst" doesn't alliterate at all. So the Danish reads as Hvidt's own rendering of his own motto rather than a translation of it, which is what the gesture is: the man restating his October sentence in his own tongue, for himself, under his signature.

**3. The undertone rhymes without stating.** Frimodighed is the Danish Bible's word for parrhesia (the 1948 NT current in 1983 — "lad os med frimodighed træde frem," approach the throne boldly). The war ends over Advent; the chaplains are on the movement schedule; the relief attacked on Second Advent. A 1983 Danish general reaching for the Bible-word in his one private inscription is period-true and character-true, and no reader is winked at — the resonance is entirely below the waterline, recoverable only by a Danish reader, which is exactly the depth-tier where the book keeps its rewards (the tin, the homework on the blackboard).

**4. The differential reader cost is zero.** The non-Danish reader finds both words equally opaque and the sentence's meaning is carried by its English twin from ch. 2 either way. Only the Danish reader is affected, and for them *ærlighed* is flat while *frimodighed* is marked — a word someone chose.

**The counter-brief, honestly:** Hvidt is a plain man, and *frimodighed* is a marked, slightly elevated word — you could argue the flattest word is the most Hvidt. I think the counter fails on the page: the inscription is already his one marked gesture of the entire war (four handwritten words, in Danish, on a NATO document, unexplained forever). The one ornament can bear the one resonant word; plainness lives in "Betalt fuldt ud," which stays.

Ripple: none — the phrase occurs exactly once in canon, the English "candor before comfort" is untouched everywhere, and the timeline ledger's banked flag closes with the ruling. Say the word and it lands as a one-line edit.

Frimodighed is in canon (8184344), and the full title survey is filed at planning/title-brief.md (524bab9). The shape of it:

**Four tests first:** don't promise the wrong book (no Red-Storm register); survive the shelf; carry the book's *question*, not its answer (The Goal's title names the thing the book spends itself discovering — ours should sit at that depth); and if "mission" stays, the enemy-side asymmetry must be consciously ratified, because it's Kuragin's word, the directive's word, never blue's — blue spends the whole book unable to answer in that vocabulary, which is the point.

**The leaderboard:**

1. **The Mission** — ratify, with a subtitle. It passes the question-test best of the whole field precisely *because* of the asymmetry: naming the book in the enemy's vocabulary makes the title itself ask "whose?" The Goal rhyme (definite article + abstract noun) is legible to exactly the readers who'd care, and your agent-reading — the agent must determine what the user's mission is — stays below the waterline. The price is the 1986 Joffé film owning the search results; a subtitle pays most of it.
2. **The Baltic Approaches** — strongest challenger. The theater's real name with a free second reading ("approaches" = ways of approaching a problem), dignified, honest. And the trimmed variant **The Approaches** has one elegant property: Plate I already bears that title, so the front matter would open on the book's name drawn as a map.
3. **According to Plan** — the double-ledger irony: Zawadzki's "no sentence in the language I trust less, and this morning it happens to be true," plus blue's own ratified correction. Both armies in three words.
4. **Green Against What** — the epistemology title; riskiest and most distinctive; the small-literary-press pick.
5. **Candor Before Comfort** — the ethic verbatim, with its hazard priced: it smells of the leadership-fable shelf. Devil's advocate: that is The Goal's *actual* shelf, so the "wrong" shelf is the ancestral one.

**Notables from the deep bench:** **Solvent** (one word, the thesis — but it names the answer, violating test 3); **The Second Column** (a genuinely rich triple: accounting column / the enemy *is* a marching column / the buy side of the page — but a puzzle-box); **Until** (the ch. 1 fishbone — the most inside title possible, precious to a fault); **Ninety Hours** (rejected for promising a countdown thriller the book refuses to be).

**The ordered sure-to-rejects, duly included:** Throughput, Operation HOLDFAST-style op-names, Frimodighed (just spent inside the book, where it belongs), The Morning Market, Paid in Full, ENDEX, The Wall (Berlin misdirection is fatal in a Cold War novel), Danevirke, The Instrument (the AI resonance is better left unlit).

Agreed on the rename discipline — it's recorded in the brief: repo, directory, Makefile output names, and artifact links all move as the *final* action after your ruling, since the working title is load-bearing in the tooling in ways a mid-stream rename would break quietly.

The ruling is recorded in the title brief: **contenders banked until final assembly** — The Approaches and trimmed variants, The Mission, Candor Before Comfort; everything else off the board, the op-name style staying rejected with your Schleswig-Holstein-as-holdfast reading noted for the record (a pun with a historical witness, it turns out — the real 1960 joint exercise was named HOLD FAST, which suggests some BAOR staff officer got there first).

Two assembly notes filed with the ruling: the subtitle question changes shape under the new title — "A novel of November 1983" already does the period work, and "Baltic Approaches" does the theater work the old incumbent needed a subtitle to do — and Plate I's THE APPROACHES now shares the title's root, which wants either ratifying (the front matter opens on the book's name drawn as a map) or varying.

One observation for the contention file, since it struck me while landing the switch: the new title quietly keeps the asymmetry property that made The Mission defensible. "The Baltic Approaches" is *blue's* institutional vocabulary — the theater's own name, the founding directive's phrase Rahn dismantles at the reception ("It is a fine sentence and it belongs to the fleet") — so where The Mission named the book in the enemy's frame, this names it in the corps' own unexamined one, the phrase the whole first chapter proves nobody can cash. Either title is a question wearing a noun; they just interrogate different speakers. That's now in the brief for the final ruling.

## ASSISTANT

All recorded (4c11cdd). Taking your three items in turn:

**On the 23-year problem — I think the caveat cuts the other way for us, and I've put the argument on the record.** Canon never claims the HOLD FAST recommendation was *implemented*; it depicts the opposite — the Territorial screen "none of them the corps' to command" in ch. 3, the Reinfeld message, and the seam closed ad hoc by Witzel's week-two order. What we need the evidence to show is "problem identified, recommendation made, never acted on" — and that's exactly what the record gives: the 1960 recommendation plus the 1970 orbat still listing the home-guard brigade as *not under NATO command*. The 23-year gap isn't a weakness in the warrant; it *is* the warrant — institutional non-fix is the book's own subject. What the passage can't prove is that nothing fixed it quietly by 1983 specifically, so the grade is plausible-uncontradicted, which is where this book wants its invented seams.

**MCM-102-68 closed** — the footnote's "(draft) 18-12-68" explains everything: a draft Military Committee Memorandum, one year after MC 67/2, never digitized. The scrape didn't garble it; the archives just don't hold it. Annotation resolved in both reference files.

**On the Starfighters — not only plausible, it's the historically exact platform.** The Marineflieger's MFG 2 flew the F-104G in the maritime strike role until 1986 (only MFG 1 had converted to Tornado by 1983), and the naval Starfighters carried the Marineflieger's *nuclear* strike commitment — so a September 1983 SEP BARD BELL test flown with Starfighters is precisely what the period order of battle predicts; Danish F-104Gs were still flying from Aalborg too. The "US, USA" is the book's own garble (interview-sourced — likely UK/USA or USN/USAF), noted as such.

**And the Spiegel piece was very much worth the consult** — it's contemporary coverage of HOLD FAST *itself*, now distilled to reference/hold-fast-1960.md. The best of it: the corps' direct ancestor is on the page (the Allied Corps Schleswig-Holstein — Danish division + 6th PzGren, English command language, 87/51/5 staff split, commanded by Konteradmiral Bernhard Rogge, the *Atlantis* raider captain, a sailor running the land corps); Spiegel's own verdict on the exercise is ch. 1's thesis in 1960 press German — the attacker "stayed as weak as he will never be in war," second echelon never played; the alert ladder ran under its German names (*einfacher/erhöhter Alarm* — banked in the vocabulary list); Rogge's quote on nuclear restraint is ch. 6's release friction from the actual commander's mouth — warheads requested and collected from the Americans, "officer to officer, without paperwork"; and ORANGE's 8 kT strike on the Rendsburg road bridge — rail bridge intact beside it, Blue crossing by ferry and bypass through the decay window — is an exercise-nuke on the book's own HQ town, adjudicated by table. Even the 1864 flourish for the Danish march south shows the Danevirke-year resonance was period-available rather than our imposition. The Bogason ask list is now fully discharged — nothing left on it but your leisure-tier SEP BARD BELL wording, which you've just done too.

(10) a small blind panel of two NEW reception-grade instruments (dads' book club facilitator; liberal-arts seminar on "the bureaucratic mode in military writing" alongside The Good Shepherd, The Caine Mutiny, Catch-22, Anabasis, Night Flight) — because existing instruments are "at the limits of their dynamic ranges" and blind agents can't out-research our accuracy base; (11) approve Zawadzki roughening in ch. 17 with new-source slot-ins; (12) dispatch an Opus agent to map the money-metaphor system under his stated constraint (never INCORRECT early — bad-model anchoring; incomplete or elaborately/baroquely-attention-focusing is licensed; trim early use exactly along this constraint and mechanical use after it's taught); (13) inventory all human-gated research and run any remaining agent-driven research; (17) final request: "I'm about to kick off a rich compaction as we go into possibly our final chunk of work. 2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - Provenance discipline: lineage log entries at boundaries (successor's first act = confirm boundary + log line), entity index rows drafted in-span, `make archive`/`make raw-archive`, machine-stamped wordcounts via commit-msg hook.
   - Build pipeline: `make pdf` (front matter + drafts + map plates → build/the-mission.pdf via pandoc, 178pp), `make wordcount` (narrative-only), `make manuscript`, Makefile vars TITLE / DRAFT_STAMP / METADATA (title/author/date), `$(sort $(wildcard))` C-locale chapter ordering (19b sorts between 19a and 20).
   - Holdings sync topology: holdings/ submodule origin = local sibling repo /home/dlk/workspace/the-mission-1986-private (checked-out branch rejects direct push) → fix pattern: `git -C <sibling> fetch <submodule-path> main && merge --ff-only FETCH_HEAD && push` to GitHub.
   - Tic-campaign method: census by grep before ruling; register separation (narration vs dialogue vs document/wall-text — diegetic registers exempt); ranked trim lists with do-not-touch lists; deliberate keeps recorded with defenses; gentle-hand doctrine (now in session memory as gentle-hand-tic-doctrine).
   - The money-metaphor constraint (DK): never incorrect early; incomplete/baroque-at-high-VoI licensed; mechanical narration uses thin after the system is taught (~ch. 10 completion; core by ch. 5; "solvent" withheld to last page).
   - Blind-panel design: pinned ingestion corpus (front matter + header-free manuscript + plates as rendered PDFs in scratchpad), reception-grade personas, collation against standing calibration questions.
   - Research verdict grammar: CONFIRMS/EXTENDS/CONTRADICTS/SILENT/GARBLED-CHECK-PRINT; PLAUSIBLE-UNCONTRADICTED as the desired grade for invented seams.
   - BALTAP architecture (print-confirmed): SACEUR→CINCNORTH(Kolsås)→COMBALTAP(Karup, always Danish, dashed direct SACEUR line)→COMAIRBALTAP(Karup, permanently Danish)/COMNAVBALTAP(Kiel-Holtenau)/COMLANDJUTLAND(Rendsburg, alternating DA/GE)/COMLANDZEALAND(kbh.).
   - NATO alert system: MC 67 series (MC 67/2, 8 Dec 1967); graduated pre-agreed measures menu; "sixteen governments" exact for Nov 1983 (Spain joined May 1982); einfacher/erhöhter Alarm attested 1960; Danish Counter-Surprise ORANGE/SCARLET.
   - Period aviation: Marineflieger MFG 2 flew F-104G (nuclear/maritime strike) until 1986; MFG 1 converted to Tornado 1981–82; Danish F-104Gs at Aalborg.

3. Files and Code Sections:
   - **drafts/18-the-crest.md** — DK's three catches cut: "tables without slack are promises..." → ends "at his own initiative."; "the way men watch money arrive" → "only standing, and watching."; specific-increment line → "got quieter."; "valve's grammar, third conjugation" → "valve's grammar" (preserves 19a's third-count: ch. 8 feed / 18–19 attack / 19a rear-battle).    - **drafts/20-december.md** — gales recast: "died in the December gales, which arrived four days ahead of every forecast in either alliance"; motto now "Frimodighed før trøst. Betalt fuldt ud."

   - **drafts/09-the-morning-market.md** — decompression: two-Danes sentence added ("Two Danes kept the stall: the theater commander, who made the division and whom the customers met only as its consequences; and his air commander, whose conference turned the division into sorties, and whose voice was the one the corps knew."); "theater commander, a tired Danish voice" → "air commander..."; allocation pause added ("went away from the microphone; the net carried half a minute of bunker room-tone... a division being walked somewhere for a signature"); "particular lightness" → "lightness".
   - **drafts/15-sequencing.md** — bunker seats both commanders (theater commander "a Danish lieutenant general... kept himself for the weighing" — was "rates of exchange"); maritime desk embodied ("a German Kapitän zur See down from Kiel-Holtenau, the navies' seat at a Danish table"); beech-wood ending flattened (cut "did not need to be told... kept twice").
   - **drafts/02-handover.md** — alliance catalog added ("The alliance kept a catalog for such weeks, graduated, every measure agreed in advance by sixteen governments — and every numbered measure therefore a signal... the enemy as the catalog's first reader.    - **drafts/06-the-locked-door.md** — DALGAS sharpener in Aakjær's receipts: "untouched, and his maps have carried them for twenty years — every autumn our own umpires burn that line in the first week, and he has not."
   - **drafts/14-the-cathedral-rebuilt.md** — "declined to remain on deposit" → "declined to be there when he spent it"; ending flattened to "He left. The sheet stayed empty."

The complete read is done — all 22 chapters in narrative order (01–18, 19, 19a, 19b, 20), 50,428 words. The White Buffalo production probe is still running; I'll report its findings when it lands.

**Read verdict: the text reads final-assembly-ready.** This is the first full pass since every open family closed, and nothing surfaced that demands action. What I checked and noted:

- **Continuity held everywhere I pressure-tested it.** The war clock arithmetic (060955Z start; hour 38 midnight conference; hour 42 attack = 080351 Tuesday) reconciles. The crest sequence is consistent across 18/19: the model's committed-past-recovery line crossed at 0100, the actual crest early "exactly as far inside the error band as Roloff's pinned sentence had warned." The three conjugations of the valve's grammar (ch. 8 feed / 18–19 attack / 19a rear-battle) land as built.
- **The recent surgery is invisible at reading pace.** The 19→19a seam ("Daylight changed none of it") now reads as intended — party pictures, then the same space the next morning. 19b sits right where it should: the enemy's mirror-chapter to 16, and "The map, for the first time in the war, was merely correct" earns its place as the Front-side exit. The ch. 7 civil column, the ch. 9/15 decompression, and Zawadzki's discount all read as native tissue.
- **One line to put on your radar, not a flag:** 19a's "It was the only sentence about himself he was ever heard to say in that building, and it is recorded here." The "recorded here" is the book's archival-narrator stance at its most explicit (same family as "the report survives," "his radio log, preserved"). I read it as a deliberate keep — the one place the document-voice admits it *is* a document, spent on Roloff, which is the right place to spend it — but since it's the register's loudest instance, you should ratify it consciously at final read.
- **Both trimmings** are now recorded in the title brief's ruling block (*The Approaches* and article-less *Baltic Approaches* explicitly in play alongside the full form, The Mission, and Candor Before Comfort).

# White Buffalo → The Baltic Approaches: production/assembly delta report

Scope note: White Buffalo (WB) is a plated, illustration-heavy novella whose assembly logic lives in a 730-line `assemble.py`, not the Makefile. The-mission-1986 (TM) is text-with-maps and currently builds straight from the Makefile via pandoc. Many WB mechanisms are image-plumbing TM will never need; I flag those as SKIP and concentrate on the typography/composition layer, which is almost entirely borrowable and mostly missing from TM today.

Key shared fact that makes lifting cheap: **both books are set in TeX Gyre Pagella at 5.5×8.5in, 11pt, `--top-level-division=chapter`, xelatex.** WB's preamble was tuned against exactly this geometry, so its LaTeX drops in with no re-derivation.

Files of record:
- WB build brain: `/home/dlk/workspace/white-buffalo/assemble.py` (esp. lines 556–691, the pandoc invocation + `header-includes`), `/home/dlk/workspace/white-buffalo/Makefile`, `/home/dlk/workspace/white-buffalo/planning/assembly.md`, `/home/dlk/workspace/white-buffalo/drafts/00-front-matter.md`
- TM current state: `/home/dlk/workspace/the-mission-1986/Makefile`, `/home/dlk/workspace/the-mission-1986/apparatus/front-matter.md`, `/home/dlk/workspace/the-mission-1986/apparatus/latex-header.tex`

---

## 1. Build pipeline

**(a) WB does:** Three artifacts from one source tree via `make book` → `assemble.py`: `white-buffalo.pdf` (canonical), `white-buffalo-pod.pdf` (POD setting), `white-buffalo.epub`, plus Makefile targets for `docx`, `manuscript` (concatenated md), and a per-chapter `wordcount`. PDF geometry and the entire composition preamble are passed as pandoc `-V` flags and one giant `header-includes` string (assemble.py 573–691). POD production adds page-surgery targets in the Makefile:

```make
print-interior: book
	@pdfseparate -f 3 build/white-buffalo-pod.pdf 'build/.pi-%d.pdf'
	@pdfunite $$(ls build/.pi-*.pdf | sort -t- -k2 -n) build/white-buffalo-print-interior.pdf
	@rm -f build/.pi-*.pdf
	@pdfinfo build/white-buffalo-print-interior.pdf | grep Pages
```

Toolchain (flake.nix): `poppler-utils` for PDF QA (pdftoppm/pdfinfo), `vale` for prose linting, `git-lfs`.

**(b) TM does:** Single `make pdf` → straight pandoc call of `$(FRONT_MATTER) $(SOURCES)`. No epub, no docx, no assemble.py, no POD/print-interior variant. TM's `PDF_OPTS` is spartan:

```make
PDF_OPTS = $(PANDOC_OPTS) --pdf-engine=xelatex \
           --top-level-division=chapter \
           -V documentclass=book -V classoption=oneside \
           -V geometry:paperwidth=5.5in -V geometry:paperheight=8.5in \
           -V geometry:margin=0.75in -V fontsize=11pt \
           -V mainfont="$(BODYFACE)" \
           --include-in-header=apparatus/latex-header.tex
```

TM already has poppler-utils, qpdf, vale, git-lfs in its flake — the QA/surgery toolchain is present and unused. TM has one thing WB lacks and should keep: `BODYFACE` as a single source of truth shared by the PDF build and the map renderer (Makefile 40, map labels re-letter to match the body face).

**(c) Recommendations:**
- **BORROW — move composition into a header file, not inline flags.** TM's `apparatus/latex-header.tex` currently only does `\usepackage{graphicx}`. Grow it into WB's full preamble (see §4). Reason: the interior is undesigned right now; WB already solved this exact page.
- **ADAPT — decide whether TM needs an assemble.py at all.** WB needed one only for content-addressed plate injection. TM's maps are already hand-placed via raw LaTeX in `front-matter.md`, so a straight pandoc call may suffice. BUT if TM adopts the two-pass List-of-Maps or a measured colophon (§2), it will need a driver script or a second pandoc pass. Recommend a small `assemble.py` only if the two-pass features are wanted; otherwise keep the Makefile-only build.
- **BORROW — `docx` and `epub` targets** are one-liners in WB's Makefile and cost nothing:
  ```make
  epub: pandoc $(SOURCES) --toc --toc-depth=1 ...
  docx: pandoc $(SOURCES) --reference-doc=reference/template.docx ...
  ```
  Reason: a docx is the standard submission/beta-reader format; free to add.
- **SKIP for now — the POD `print-interior`/`pdfseparate` surgery.** Only relevant once there's a real POD cover leaf to strip. Bank it; the pattern is above when needed.
- **KEEP — TM's `BODYFACE` single-source-of-truth pattern.** WB has no equivalent; it's a genuine TM improvement worth preserving through any refactor.

## 2. Front matter and back matter

**(a) WB does:** A layered front matter, most of it generated:
- **Title page** rendered by a `\renewcommand{\maketitle}` (letterspaced trade styling, no rules — see §4), fed from pandoc metadata (`title`, `subtitle=A novella`, `author`, `date=2026`).
- **Half-title (p.1) → cover plate as frontispiece facing title (p.2) → title → notices spread → List of Illustrations on its own recto.** Page architecture is explicit: front matter unnumbered, `\pagenumbering{arabic}` reset so folio 1 = Chapter One forced recto (`\cleardoublepage`).
- **The "notices" (WB's copyright page in function)** live in `drafts/00-front-matter.md` and carry, as running prose set small/flush-left: a **tradition/sensitivity note**, an **AI-disclosure note**, a **working-record/repo URL**, an **attribution-review clause** ("each session was given the opportunity to review… and amend this notice"), and the **© / "First edition · ISBN TK · imprint TK"** stub.
- **Back matter:** a generated **colophon** (`build/after-body.tex`), deliberately *additive* to the front notices, never a relocation: "Set in TeX Gyre Pagella. / The frontispiece and eighteen plates were drawn by an image model… curated by the authors and audited against the text. / Assembled from the working record at github.com/…". The POD colophon adds one honest sentence about the fold-out reduction. Book closes on a blank verso via parity math.
- **List of Illustrations** is built by a **two-pass build**: build once to measure the physical page each plate lands on (`pdfimages -list` + locate "Chapter One" for the folio offset), inject a dotted-leader list with real folios, build again (assemble.py 400–456, 693–700).

**(b) TM does:** A single hand-written `apparatus/front-matter.md`: title, "*A novel of November 1983*" subtitle, `&amp;nbsp;` spacers, byline "**Daniel Klein with Claude**", then a strong **"How this book was made"** disclosure section (this is well-developed — it already covers the model/human division of labor, the public repo + attribution ledger, the per-session review-and-dissent clause, and the "ledger, not the byline, is the honest unit of account" framing). Then `\newpage` and the two map plates as raw `\includegraphics`. **No colophon, no copyright/© page, no ISBN stub, no List of Maps, no explicit half-title/frontispiece architecture, no folio/recto discipline.** The byline was formerly three options; it's now resolved to "Daniel Klein with Claude."

**(c) Recommendations:**
- **BORROW — a back-matter colophon.** TM has none. WB's is a clean template; TM's equivalent writes itself: "Set in TeX Gyre Pagella. The two plates — *The Approaches* and *The Neck* — were drawn from 1983 transport-network data and public-domain base geography by the project's atlas. Assembled from the working record at github.com/…". Reason: it's the natural home for the maps' provenance and the repo pointer, and it balances the front disclosure.
- **BORROW — a functional copyright/notices page with the © + "First edition · ISBN TK · imprint TK" stub.** TM's front matter has the disclosure argument but no copyright line at all. WB's exact stub (`© 2026 … All rights reserved… First edition · ISBN TK · imprint TK`) is directly liftable. Reason: even a pre-publication book wants the notices page to exist as a page; TK placeholders are the honest way to hold it open.
- **ADAPT — WB's "notice set small, flush-left, block-spaced" treatment** (assemble.py 481–488: `\begingroup\small\setlength{\parindent}{0pt}\setlength{\parskip}{0.6\baselineskip}…\endgroup`). TM's disclosure is currently body prose; setting it as *notices* (smaller, flush-left) visually distinguishes apparatus from narrative and reads as more deliberate. Reason: TM's "How this book was made" is load-bearing and should not look like chapter one.
- **CONSIDER — WB's attribution-review clause is stronger phrased in TM already** ("any dissent is recorded verbatim… and, if unresolved at publication, acknowledged on this page. As of this draft, none has been filed…"). No change needed; noting that TM has independently reached WB's provenance-disclosure bar. This is the "how did a human+AI collab disclose itself" answer for both: *disclosure precedes reading, on the copyright page, with a repo pointer and a session-review clause* — not tucked in a back-matter author's note.
- **BORROW IF maps get a reference-list — the two-pass folio-measurement trick** for a "List of Maps / Plates with page numbers" (assemble.py `plate_folios` + `illustrations_list`). Reason: TM's own status.md (line 782) ratifies a reader-facing map program; if the maps ever want a titled entry with a folio, WB's `pdfimages -list` + "find Chapter One offset" two-pass is the mechanism. SKIP if the two front-matter maps stay unlisted (likely fine at two plates).
- **SKIP — half-title + frontispiece-facing-title architecture.** WB needs it because it has a cover plate to face the title; TM's front is maps, not a frontispiece. TM's simpler title→disclosure→maps order is appropriate.

## 3. Chapter presentation

**(a) WB does:** **Unnumbered chapters** — every H1 gets `{.unnumbered}` injected once per file (assemble.py 505), so there's no "Chapter 1" furniture; the chapter *title* stands alone. Journal-entry files get their H1 rewritten `# Journal Entry N` → `# From the Journal — N` at assembly (491–503). **Scene breaks** are asterisms, not rules: `---` → `\scenebreak` (a centered `* * *`). **First-line indents, no inter-paragraph space** (`-V indent=true` + `\frenchspacing`). Journal dated subheads get a keep-with-next `\JEdate` device. **No running heads** — considered and left as a taste call (assembly.md line 58). `openany` kept deliberately (a plated novella would scatter blank versos otherwise).

**(b) TM does:** Chapter headers are `# 1 — ENDEX` (numeral + em-dash + title). With `documentclass=book` + `--top-level-division=chapter`, pandoc will render this as **"Chapter 1" auto-furniture followed by the heading text "1 — ENDEX"** — i.e. the numeral appears three ways (auto "Chapter 1", the literal "1", and again in any TOC). Scene breaks are `---` thematic breaks (confirmed: 4 in ch.1, ~50 across the book) which pandoc renders as a **horizontal rule** — WB explicitly calls the bare rule its "loudest LaTeX tell." No running heads. TM uses `classoption=oneside`.

**(c) Recommendations:**
- **BORROW NOW — the `\scenebreak` asterism transform.** This is the single highest-value, lowest-effort lift. TM's `---` currently renders as an ugly `\rule`. Replace with WB's exact device (from assemble.py 632–634):
  ```latex
  \newcommand{\scenebreak}{\par\needspace{8\baselineskip}
    \bigskip{\centering*\enspace*\enspace*\par}\nobreak\bigskip\nobreak}
  ```
  and transform `^---$` → `\scenebreak` (needs a driver script or a pandoc Lua filter, since it's a source rewrite). WB's hard-won note: reserve **8** baselines not 4, or a following keep-with-next block re-breaks the page and strands the asterism at the foot (their bug, folios 48/75/78/89/103). Reason: TM has scene breaks on nearly every chapter and they currently print as rules.
- **BORROW/DECIDE — chapter-header numeral handling.** TM's status.md (lines 775–781) has this as an *open* final-assembly decision: DK's "leading option" is title-dominant headers with the numeral demoted to TOC/running heads. WB's `{.unnumbered}` mechanism is exactly that pattern already implemented — inject `{.unnumbered}` on each chapter H1 to kill the auto "Chapter N," and restyle the title. Reason: WB has already built the thing TM is deciding whether to build; TM can lift the mechanism and just choose how to render the numeral.
- **CONSIDER — running heads.** Both left them off. TM is a longer, more navigable book (20+ chapters, corps-HQ ensemble) than a 31k novella; a running head (author verso / chapter-title recto) is more defensible here than in WB. Flag as a live taste call, not a default-off.
- **NOTE — `oneside` vs WB's mirrored-margin `book`.** TM uses `oneside` with a uniform 0.75in margin; WB uses two-sided mirrored margins with a binding gutter. See §4 — this is the biggest single interior-design gap.

---

## 4. Typography and layout

This is where TM is furthest behind and WB's work is most directly liftable. WB's entire composition preamble (assemble.py 613–648) is tuned for the identical trim/font/size TM uses.

**(a) WB does** — the load-bearing preamble, verbatim:

Geometry (mirrored margins, binding gutter, same 4.0in measure):
```
geometry:paperwidth=5.5in,paperheight=8.5in,inner=0.85in,outer=0.65in,top=0.8in,bottom=0.9in,headsep=0.18in
```
Composition (`header-includes`):
```latex
\usepackage{graphicx}\usepackage{needspace}
\frenchspacing\raggedbottom
\widowpenalty=10000\clubpenalty=10000\brokenpenalty=10000
\finalhyphendemerits=10000000
\emergencystretch=1.5em
\hyphenation{Oswego Catherine Aldridge Farrell Morrow Hardin Niobrara Monterrey Chapultepec Kearny Creighton}
```
Plus `-V indent=true` (first-line indents, no inter-paragraph space), `-f markdown+smart` (smart quotes/dashes), and a keep-with-next `\JEdate`. Title page (letterspaced, rule-free trade design — the "boundary grammar is whitespace, not furniture"):
```latex
\renewcommand{\maketitle}{\thispagestyle{empty}
  \null\vspace{0.16\textheight}
  {\centering{\addfontfeatures{LetterSpace=18}\fontsize{22}{27}\selectfont WHITE BUFFALO\par}
  \vspace{1.4\baselineskip}
  {\addfontfeatures{LetterSpace=14}\scshape a novella\par}
  \vspace{0.32\textheight}
  {\addfontfeatures{LetterSpace=8}\large BEN COHEN \normalsize\itshape and \large\upshape DANIEL KLEIN\par}
  \vfill{\addfontfeatures{LetterSpace=8}2026\par}\vspace{0.06\textheight}}\clearpage}
```

**(b) TM does:** Uniform 0.75in margins, `oneside`, `fontsize=11pt`, `mainfont=TeX Gyre Pagella`. **None of:** first-line indents (pandoc default under `book` is actually indented, but no `\raggedbottom`), widow/orphan control, `\frenchspacing`, `\emergencystretch`, proper-name hyphenation exceptions, smart-quote extension (`--from=markdown` without `+smart`), a designed title page (TM's title is just the H1 in `front-matter.md`), or mirrored margins/gutter.

**(c) Recommendations:**
- **BORROW WHOLESALE — the composition preamble** (`\frenchspacing\raggedbottom`, the three `\...penalty=10000` lines, `\finalhyphendemerits`, `\emergencystretch=1.5em`). These are trim-independent and TM wants every one of them. Reason: widows/orphans and flush-bottom blowouts are the same on any 5.5×8.5 book; WB already paid for these lines.
- **BORROW — `--from=markdown+smart`** (TM currently uses plain `markdown`). Reason: gives real typographic quotes and en/em dashes; WB relies on it and TM's em-dash-heavy prose ("ENDEX, said…") needs it.
- **ADAPT — mirrored margins vs `oneside`.** WB's `inner=0.85in,outer=0.65in` gutter is the correct choice for a bound book. TM's `oneside` + uniform 0.75in is a screen/manuscript setting. If TM intends a POD interior, switch to two-sided with WB's gutter geometry. Reason: this is the decision that most distinguishes "manuscript PDF" from "trade book." (WB kept `openany` because plates; TM without plates can use normal recto-openings — a *divergence* TM should make deliberately, not inherit.)
- **BORROW &amp; POPULATE — the `\hyphenation{…}` exception list.** TM is dense with proper nouns that xelatex will mis-break: Schleswig-Holstein, Jutland, Rendsburg, Karup, Danevirke, Frimodighed, Ærlighed, Bogason, etc. Reason: WB's list exists precisely to stop ugly proper-name breaks; TM's noun inventory is larger.
- **BORROW &amp; RESTYLE — the `\renewcommand{\maketitle}` designed title page.** TM's title is currently an untreated H1. WB's letterspaced, rule-free title page is a strong template; swap the strings (THE BALTIC APPROACHES / a novel of November 1983 / DANIEL KLEIN with CLAUDE / 1983-or-2026). Reason: TM's front matter has good *words* and no *design*; WB supplies the design. (Watch: WB uses `\addfontfeatures{LetterSpace=…}`, which requires xelatex + a font that supports it — Pagella does, so it transfers.)
- **NOTE the `\JEdate` keep-with-next pattern** (assemble.py 622–624) is journal-specific to WB, but the *technique* (`\needspace{4\baselineskip}` + `\@afterindentfalse\@afterheading` to bind a subhead to its following text with a flush first paragraph) is reusable if TM ever has dated datelines/DTG subheads inside chapters. ADAPT-if-needed.

## 5. Production checklists / QA / hard-won gotchas

**(a) WB does:** `planning/assembly.md` is a running production log with dated passes ("Production pass 2026-07-12 evening," "A2 pass 2026-07-13") recording each fix and *why*. Documented gotchas worth inheriting:
- **The bare-rule scene break was "production's loudest LaTeX tell" twice running** → asterism (and the 8-vs-4 baseline `\needspace` trap above).
- **Notices URL clipped off-trim** at v1 → forced onto its own line.
- **pandoc's line-based raw-TeX heuristic mangles brace groups** — a leaked `\scshape` once "set the whole body in smallcaps." Fix: emit any multi-line raw LaTeX as an explicit ```` ```{=latex} ```` fenced block, never bare (assemble.py 405–406, 197).
- **pandoc/template `\frontmatter`/`\mainmatter` silently reset folios** at the wrong place → neutralized with `\renewcommand{\frontmatter}{}` and explicit `\pagenumbering`.
- **Two-pass builds** because you cannot know a plate's/figure's folio until after the first typeset.
- QA render loop: `poppler-utils` (pdftoppm thumbnails) to eyeball every page; a **pre-commit hook** rebuilds the book when drafts/art/assemble change and **fails the commit on anchor drift** (`make hooks`).

**(b) TM does:** TM has an extensive planning/notes discipline (status.md, blind-reads, tic-inventory, timeline-ledger) and already caught the **markdown-glob-order lesson** ("PDF not markdown corpus" — status.md 481) and the **header/page-number extraction interleave** issue for blind panels. TM has commit-msg + pre-commit hooks (wordcount stamp, holdings guard) but **no build-QA hook and no assembly production log**. TM's final-assembly to-do already lives in status.md item 5 (19/19a renumbering, header style, byline, wordcount position, maps).

**(c) Recommendations:**
- **BORROW — start a `planning/assembly.md` production log now.** TM is entering final assembly with the checklist scattered across status.md. WB's dated-pass format ("what we changed this pass and why") is the right container. Reason: final assembly is exactly where undocumented fixes get silently reverted by the next session.
- **BORROW — the four concrete gotchas** (bare rule → asterism; raw-LaTeX must be fenced `{=latex}`; `\frontmatter` folio reset; two-pass for any page-referencing apparatus). TM's `front-matter.md` *already* uses bare raw LaTeX (`\thispagestyle`, `\includegraphics`) rather than fenced blocks — it works today because it's simple, but the moment a brace group spans lines, WB's smallcaps-leak bug is waiting. ADAPT: fence multi-line raw LaTeX.
- **BORROW — a `poppler-utils` page-render QA step.** TM already ships pdftoppm; add a `make proof` that renders trim thumbnails for eyeballing (WB's habit). Reason: interior-design bugs (stranded asterisms, bad breaks) are invisible in markdown and obvious in a page render.
- **CONSIDER — a build-QA pre-commit hook** that rebuilds the PDF when drafts/apparatus change. WB's fails on anchor drift; TM's analogue would fail if `make maps`/`make pdf` errors. Lower priority than WB's since TM has no content-addressed anchors, but cheap insurance.

## 6. Cover / plate / illustration handling

**(a) WB does:** A full image pipeline (imagegen/, LoRA training, knockout, resolution gates, cover composition + wrap-spine sizing `cover_wrap.py --pages 140`, cream-ground knockout to page white). Plates injected at content-addressed text anchors; full-page leaves with folio-counted-but-unprinted pages; a gatefold with `\pdfpagewidth` primitive tricks. All heavily bespoke.

**(b) TM does:** Two monochrome **vector** map plates (`plate-approaches.pdf`, `plate-neck.pdf`) rendered by the `atlas` module → SVG → PDF (headless Chromium via `scripts/svg2pdf.py`), placed via raw `\includegraphics` in `front-matter.md`. Plate II is rotated 90° (`angle=90`) so the reader turns the book — a deliberate, documented choice (`planning/map-spec.md`). There is a "cover stamp" referenced in status.md but the cover itself appears out of scope.

**(c) Recommendations:**
- **SKIP — essentially all of WB's raster-image plumbing** (knockout, resolution gates, LoRA, gatefold, POD spread-split, content-addressed anchor injection). TM's plates are vector, count-excluded apparatus, front-matter-fixed, and already built by a cleaner pipeline. WB's plate machinery solves problems TM doesn't have.
- **BORROW — the full-page-leaf LaTeX pattern** *if* any TM plate ever needs to be its own centered page (assemble.py 251–265):
  ```latex
  \clearpage\thispagestyle{empty}\vspace*{\fill}
  {\centering\includegraphics[width=\textwidth]{...}\par}
  \vspace*{\fill}\clearpage
  ```
  TM's front-matter maps already use a `\vspace*{\fill}…\vspace*{\fill}` centering that mirrors this — so TM independently arrived at WB's leaf pattern. No change needed; noted as convergence.
- **BORROW — `\pdfpagewidth` mixed-page-size trick** only if TM ever wants a fold-out theater map (the rotated Neck plate is the natural candidate for a landscape gatefold). Bank the technique (assemble.py 229–246); don't build it now.
- **NOTE — WB's cover_wrap spine math** (`cream bulk 0.0025 in/page`, spine sized from the print-interior page count) is the reference if TM ever produces a POD wrap cover. SKIP until there's a cover program.

The White Buffalo probe came back rich, and I've already landed the mechanical half of it. Where things stand:

**Applied and committed (pass 1, render-verified, 171pp):**
- **Scene breaks are asterisms now** — our ~50 `---` breaks were printing as bare horizontal rules, which WB's log calls production's loudest LaTeX tell. Fixed via a Lua filter (cleaner than WB's source-rewrite approach), with their hard-won 8-baseline reservation against stranded asterisms.
- **Full composition preamble** — widow/orphan/broken-hyphen penalties, `\frenchspacing`, `\raggedbottom`, `\emergencystretch`. One env gotcha: our texliveSmall lacks `needspace.sty`, so its core macro is inlined in the header.
- **Hyphenation exceptions** for ~35 recurring German/Danish nouns (Oberstløjtnant, Friedrichstadt, Vestergaard…) — English patterns won't break them, and on a 4-inch measure that's blown lines.
- **Trade paragraphs** (`indent=true`) — first-line indents instead of manuscript block-and-space. Dialogue pages now read as a novel page; saved 7 pages. WB, our ruled deliverable bar, does the same.

**Probe corrections worth knowing:** three of its claims failed verification — smart punctuation was already on, we already have running heads (book-class default), and there is no "Chapter N" auto-furniture (chapters already open title-dominant as "1 — ENDEX"). So two of the "design forks" are smaller than reported.

**The menu still open for you** (all logged in the new `planning/assembly.md`, a WB-borrowed production log):
1. **Front-matter architecture** — the real remaining Tier-1 work. Currently the title prints twice (pandoc's auto title block, then the H1 page) and the disclosure shares a page with the second title. WB's pattern: designed letterspaced title page, then a notices page (disclosure set small/flush-left + © line + "First edition · ISBN TK · imprint TK" stub), then plates. I can draft this for your eyes next.
2. **Mirrored margins + binding gutter vs. current `oneside`** — the manuscript-vs-bound-book fork; WB's geometry is directly liftable.
3. **Chapter headers** — now just: does the literal "1 —" stay in the heading, or demote to TOC/running heads only? (Ties into the 19/19a/19b renumbering.)
4. **Back-matter colophon** — natural home for the plates' provenance and the repo pointer.

Banked for later: POD page surgery, two-pass List of Maps, gatefold trick, epub/docx targets. And the probe confirmed three things we do better than WB and must not regress: the `BODYFACE` single source of truth, the draft-stamp metadata, and the disclosure page (already above WB's provenance bar).

My suggestion for order: I draft the title/notices pages next (item 1), you rule on 2–4 when you see the rendered front.

# Design review: THE BALTIC APPROACHES interior (build of 2026-07-27, 171 pp.)

## (a) Reaction to the object

**The text block is already a book; the furniture around it is still a manuscript.** That is the one-sentence review. The body composition after the Tier-1 pass is genuinely good: justification color on ordinary prose pages (folios 45, 99, 149, 154) is even and trade-grade; French spacing, the widow/orphan penalties, and the asterism device all hold — I found no widows, no orphans, no foot-stranded asterisms in a wide sample; the proper-noun hyphenation list is visibly earning its keep (`Oberst-løjtnant` breaking cleanly on folio 19, Danish diacritics — ø, æ, Skål — all setting correctly). Pagella at 11pt on the 4-inch measure is the right color for this register, and the asterism spacing (folio 6, 57, 149) is correct.

Measured against **white-buffalo**, the gap is entirely in architecture, not in composition:

- WB opens: cover leaf → half-title → letterspaced title page ("W H I T E  B U F F A L O / A NOVELLA / byline / year") → provenance note with asterism separators → © and edition stub → Illustrations list with leaders and folios → Chapter One on folio 1, front matter unfoliated. Every page has one job.
- TM opens: pandoc's `\maketitle` (small centered title + byline + draft stamp floating mid-page) → then a *second* title, set as a bold chapter-style H1, **sharing its page with "How this book was made"**, and — the loudest tell — that page is **folio 1, with the running head "THE BALTIC APPROACHES / 2" on its overflow page** (PDF p. 3, which carries five lines of disclosure and a 90% blank page). The apparatus is literally dressed as chapter one. Then the two plates, then "1 — ENDEX" opening on folio 5.

Real defects I can see in the renders, with pages (PDF page / printed folio):

1. **p. 2–3 (folios 1–2):** disclosure styled as a chapter, foliated, running-headed, with a nearly blank overflow page. The single most amateur spread in the book.
2. **p. 1:** duplicate title page (auto-`\maketitle`), title at text size — under-designed even as a draft cover.
3. **Caps message passages hyphenate:** "CLO-/SURE" (p. 30), "PRI-/ORITY" (p. 59), "CAL-/ENDAR" (p. 104). A teleprinter never hyphenates; every one of these breaks the fiction the device exists to create. Justified full-size caps also produce gappy lines (p. 104 top block is the worst).
4. **p. 57 (folio 56):** visibly short page — the `\needspace{8\baselineskip}` before the next scene break pushed the asterism to p. 58 and `\raggedbottom` left ~8 blank lines. Correct behavior, visible cost; acceptable, but this is the page to re-check after reflow.
5. **Top-of-page asterisms** at p. 123 and p. 150 (directly under the running head). This is standard practice for marked scene breaks (the cue must survive the page turn) — not a defect, but note it's the flip side of the needspace design.
6. **Running heads are book-class defaults**: slanted full caps "1 — ENDEX" duplicating the numeral that is already in the displayed heading and the folio — the number appears in two places on every spread. "19A — THE POCKET" (p. 154+) shows how badly the letter-suffix chapters uppercase.
7. **No half-title, no contents, no copyright page, no colophon, no gutter.** The book starts and simply stops (p. 171 ends mid-page, then nothing).

None of this is expensive. The hard part — the text block — is done.

## (b) Rulings on the five forks

### 1. Front-matter architecture — rebuild to the WB pattern, seven leaves

Recommended sequence (folios: front matter carries **no expressed folios**; arabic 1 starts at Chapter 1):

- **i — Half-title.** Title alone, upper third, letterspaced caps at modest size (`\large`, `LetterSpace=14`). `\thispagestyle{empty}`.
- **ii — blank.**
- **iii — Title page.** The WB design with strings swapped: `T H E  B A L T I C  A P P R O A C H E S` (letterspaced, ~20pt), below it small letterspaced caps `A NOVEL OF NOVEMBER 1983`, byline `DANIEL KLEIN with CLAUDE` letterspaced caps at mid-page-lower, year at foot. No rules, no bold.
- **iv — Notices page** (verso of title, the traditional copyright position). Set `\small`, flush left, `\parindent 0pt`: the full "How this book was made" disclosure (drop its H2 heading to a letterspaced small-caps line, not bold); then a centered `* * *` separator (echoing WB p. 7 and your own scene device); then the © line; then `First edition · ISBN TK · imprint TK`; then — while the book is in draft — the provenance stamp `Draft three · 2026-07-27 · 269b3dd` as the last line. At `\small` on your text block the disclosure fits with the stubs on one page; if it ever overflows, let it run to v and move plates back one leaf.
- **v — Contents** (recto). See the TOC ruling in (c), item 3 — yes, include one.
- **vi–vii — the map plates as a facing spread**: Plate I (The Approaches) on the verso, Plate II (The Neck, broadside) on the recto. Once mirrored margins land (fork 2), this gives the reader both maps open at once immediately before the text — the strongest argument for exactly this placement. Keep `\thispagestyle{empty}`.
- **Chapter 1 opens on the next recto, folio 1.**

Kill pandoc's `\maketitle` entirely (`-V title=""` won't do it cleanly; the right move is dropping the `title` metadata from `METADATA` and carrying title/byline only in `-V title-meta`/`-V author-meta` or a `\hypersetup`-free `\special` so PDF metadata survives — WB's `assemble.py` has the working pattern). The draft stamp's provenance function moves to the notices page; do not run two title pages in parallel. Neutralize `\frontmatter`/`\mainmatter` folio resets per the gotcha already in assembly.md, and set `\pagenumbering{arabic}` explicitly at the first chapter.

Tradeoff: +4–5 leaves of front matter. That is what a trade book costs; WB paid it.

### 2. Margins — go two-sided, mirrored, with the WB values; and take openright

Rule: **adopt** `twoside` with `inner=0.85in, outer=0.65in, top=0.8in, bottom=0.9in` (drop `classoption=oneside`; geometry lines: `-V geometry:inner=0.85in -V geometry:outer=0.65in -V geometry:top=0.8in -V geometry:bottom=0.9in`). This keeps your current 4.0in measure exactly (0.85+0.65 = 1.5 = the current 0.75×2), so line breaks barely move; only the vertical block shrinks 0.2in. The deliverable bar is a bound paperback; uniform 0.75in margins print with text falling into the gutter. This is the manuscript→book decision and there is no argument for staying `oneside` except screen reading — for which `build/the-mission.pdf` as a *reading* draft could retain a separate oneside target if DK wants (cheap Makefile variant), but the canonical build should be mirrored.

**openright: yes** — and log it as a deliberate divergence from WB, exactly as assembly.md anticipates: WB kept `openany` because of interleaved plates; you have none in the body, so take the recto-opening rhythm of a trade novel. Cost: ~8–11 blank versos on 22 chapters, call it +10 pages on ~175. Two implementation notes: (a) `book` + `openright` is the default once `oneside` is dropped — you need no new option; (b) blank versos must be truly blank — `emptypage.sty` is latexextra (not in texliveSmall), so inline the standard `\cleardoublepage` redefinition that stamps `\thispagestyle{empty}` on the inserted blank. Sequence matters: **do the reflow before any page-referenced apparatus (TOC folios) is generated** — two-pass, per your own banked note.

### 3. Chapter headers — demote the numeral to a small overline deck; strip it from the H1 text

The current `1 — ENDEX` at `\Huge` bold makes the em dash a display-size graphic element and welds bookkeeping to the title; at p. 112 the long title wraps ("14 — The Cathedral, / Rebuilt" per the text layer) with the numeral stranded on the first line. Rule: **the displayed numeral survives, but demoted** — the trade-standard two-deck:

```
CHAPTER 4            &lt;- \footnotesize, letterspaced caps (LetterSpace=15), flush left
Norms                &lt;- \huge \bfseries, flush left, as now
```

Cheapest correct implementation, given clean 1..22 renumbering: a Lua filter strips the leading `^%d+[ab]? — ` from every chapter H1, and the build adds `--number-sections`; LaTeX's own `\@makechapterhead` then produces exactly this two-deck ("Chapter 4" over the title), numbers the TOC, and feeds `\chaptermark` — one filter, zero new packages. Restyle the "Chapter N" line later (a 10-line `\@makechapterhead` patch, or the `\tmchapter{n}{Title}` macro route) if the default furniture line reads too generic; but the default *is* the trade convention and is a large step up from the em-dash form.

Counter-brief, honestly stated: the `N — TITLE` form has a ledger-entry flavor that suits this book's ethos, and DK could ratify it as a device. But it must then be defended in the running heads and the TOC too, and "at display size, bold, with a wrapping title" is where it stops looking like a device and starts looking like a filename. I recommend against.

### 4. Running heads — keep them, restyle; do not drop to folio-only

WB went folio-only, defensibly, at 31k words and unnumbered journal-interleaved chapters. This book is the opposite case: 22 titled chapters, ensemble cast, procedural texture — running heads are functional here, and the titles are good ("The Narrows", "Sequencing"). Rule: **keep heads, replace the book-class default** (slanted caps + duplicated numeral) with:

- verso: folio at outer margin, book title centered-or-inner in letterspaced small caps
- recto: chapter title (no numeral) in letterspaced small caps, folio at outer margin
- chapter-opening pages and all front matter: no head; openings get folio centered in the foot (`plain`), fronts get `empty`.

`fancyhdr` is in texliveSmall (latexrecommended). Concretely:

```latex
\usepackage{fancyhdr}
\newcommand{\rhfont}{\footnotesize\scshape\addfontfeature{LetterSpace=8}}
\pagestyle{fancy}\fancyhf{}
\renewcommand{\headrulewidth}{0pt}
\renewcommand{\chaptermark}[1]{\markboth{#1}{}}
\fancyhead[LE]{\thepage\hspace{1.5em}{\rhfont\MakeLowercase{The Baltic Approaches}}}
\fancyhead[RO]{{\rhfont\MakeLowercase{\leftmark}}\hspace{1.5em}\thepage}
```

(`\MakeLowercase` inside `\scshape` yields even small caps; letterspace small caps, never bold.) Tradeoff versus folio-only: a little more ink per page against real wayfinding; in an austere design the letterspaced small-cap line reads as apparatus, which is on-register for this book.

### 5. Back-matter colophon — yes; final recto (or the recto after the last text page)

Rule: add it. It balances the front disclosure (making = front, materials = back, the classic split), and WB generates one, so the house style already expects it. Content exactly as proposed: set-in note ("Set in TeX Gyre Pagella, 11 on 13.6, composed with pandoc and XeLaTeX"), the plates' provenance (drawn from the project's transport atlas of the 1983 Baltic Approaches; base geometry Natural Earth, public domain), and the pointer to the public working record (repo URL; note the attribution ledger by name — it is the book's thesis). Styling: `\cleardoublepage` onto a recto, `\thispagestyle{empty}`, vertically centered or upper-third, `\small`, either centered lines or flush-left like the notices page; a letterspaced small-caps `A NOTE ON THE MAKING OF THIS BOOK` head, or headless with a `* * *` above — reuse the asterism, tie the device closed. Cheap: one markdown/raw-TeX file appended in `SOURCES` order (e.g. `apparatus/back-matter.md` added after `$(SOURCES)` in the pandoc call).

## (c) Punch list (ranked; cheap / moderate / expensive)

1. **Front-matter rebuild per fork 1** — kills defects #1, #2, and the folio/running-head leakage on pp. 2–3 in one stroke. *Moderate* (it's the designed title page + notices layout; everything else is rearrangement).
2. **Mirrored margins + openright per fork 2** — do first, since everything reflows. *Cheap* (option/geometry flags + the `\cleardoublepage` blank-verso fix).
3. **Add a Contents page.** Currently absent; with 22 titled chapters in a document-inflected book, a contents list is both useful and on-register (The Caine Mutiny carries one). One line per chapter, number + title + folio; either WB's dotted leaders (matching its Illustrations page) or, more austere, a 1em space and old-style folio, no leaders. `--toc` gets you the mechanism; restyle `\tableofcontents` spacing minimally; remember the two-pass folio rule. *Cheap–moderate.*
4. **Teleprinter/message treatment.** The signature texture, currently defaulting. Minimum viable fix, worth doing: for the *set-off* message paragraphs (folio 103's evening estimate, 122's 23 NOV assessment, 29's closing report), drop to `\small`, add `LetterSpace=3`, set `\hyphenpenalty=10000 \exhyphenpenalty=10000`, and set ragged right — no hyphenated CLOSURE, no gappy justified caps, and the block reads as a different paper stock, which is the point. Requires marking the blocks in source (fenced div + Lua filter → a `messageblock` environment), which is the real cost. Leave short *inline* caps runs (SCHEDULED H+90 etc.) exactly as they are — they are speech-adjacent and the caps alone carry them. Do **not** reach for a monospace face; it breaks the single-face austerity and the BODYFACE single-source rule. *Moderate* (the markup pass across ~10 chapters is the work).
5. **Chapter heading two-deck + running-head restyle per forks 3–4.** *Cheap* once renumbering lands (one Lua strip + `--number-sections` + the fancyhdr block).
6. **Colophon per fork 5.** *Cheap.*
7. **Ch. 7 letter (folio 56).** Currently an ordinary quoted paragraph. Defensible as-is — it is recalled ("he had it by heart"), so quotation-as-memory is a literary choice, and I would not force a change. If DK wants the letter to read as a *document* like the caps traffic does: block-indent both margins 1.5em, drop the quotation marks, size unchanged, roman not italic (a one-page letter in italic is a romance-novel tell; this letter is "the household's order of battle"). *Cheap; taste call — put it to DK with both options.*
8. **Plate spread.** Keep Plate II broadside — the rotation direction is already correct (reader turns the book clockwise; top of image to the spine). Under fork 1, place the plates as a facing spread (I verso / II recto). Plate I fills the 4in measure and its labels sit at the legibility floor but above it; if anything, revisit the plate's internal margins in the atlas renderer to buy 5% more map, not the page layout. *Cheap (placement) / moderate (re-rendering plates).*
9. **Post-reflow proof pass.** After items 1–2 re-break every page: re-run the render loop (`make proof` per the banked WB pattern) checking specifically for foot/top asterisms, short pages like current p. 57, and caps-block breaks. *Cheap, mandatory.*
10. **Microtype protrusion.** `\usepackage{microtype}` (in texliveSmall; protrusion works under XeTeX, expansion does not) — slightly better optical margins for free. *Cheap.*
11. **PDF metadata hygiene at print time:** title/author are correct; the epoch-zero CreationDate is a reproducibility artifact, fine for now; revisit only for distribution. *Cheap, defer.*

**What not to touch:** the body face, size, and measure; the asterism device (including its top-of-page appearances); `\raggedbottom` with the current penalties (the p. 57 short page is the honest price and the right trade); the hyphenation exception list; the disclosure's *text* (it is above WB's bar — it just needs to stop being dressed as chapter one).

Bottom line: composition is at the reference bar today; architecture is roughly a day of work behind it, and every item above except the teleprinter markup pass and the title-page design is a flag-level or ten-line change.</result>

## ASSISTANT

The review is in and banked (`planning/assembly.md`, commit 3f1150d). Its one-sentence verdict: **"The text block is already a book; the furniture around it is still a manuscript."** Composition was judged at the White Buffalo bar — no widows, orphans, or stranded asterisms found in a wide sample — and everything it wants is architecture. Here's the menu.

**Defects it saw in the renders (I endorse fixing all of these):**
- The disclosure page is dressed as chapter one — foliated, running-headed, with a near-blank overflow page. Its words: the single most amateur spread in the book.
- The title prints twice (pandoc's undesigned `\maketitle` page, then the H1).
- **The teleprinter passages hyphenate** — CLO-/SURE (p.30), PRI-/ORITY (p.59), CAL-/ENDAR (p.104). "A teleprinter never hyphenates." The justified full-size caps also go gappy.
- Default running heads duplicate the chapter numeral, and "19A — THE POCKET" uppercases badly.

**Its rulings on the five forks:**
1. **Front matter** — rebuild to the WB seven-leaf pattern: half-title / blank / designed letterspaced title page / notices verso (disclosure at `\small` flush-left, © line, ISBN-TK stub, draft stamp as last line) / Contents recto / **the two map plates as a facing spread** (Approaches verso, Neck broadside recto — both maps open at once just before the text) / Chapter 1 on a recto at folio 1, front matter unfoliated.
2. **Margins** — adopt WB's mirrored two-sided geometry (inner 0.85 / outer 0.65; our 4.0in measure is preserved exactly, so line breaks barely move) **plus openright** chapter openings, logged as a deliberate divergence from WB (~+10pp of blank versos). Optional cheap `oneside` Makefile variant kept for screen reading.
3. **Chapter headers** — demote the numeral to a two-deck ("CHAPTER 4" small letterspaced caps over the title). It stated the counter-brief honestly: `N — TITLE` has a ledger-entry flavor you could ratify as a device, but at display size with wrapping titles ("14 — The Cathedral, / Rebuilt") "it stops looking like a device and starts looking like a filename."
4. **Running heads** — keep but restyle (folio-only rejected for a 22-chapter procedural): letterspaced small caps, book title verso / chapter title recto, no numerals, never bold.
5. **Colophon** — yes, final recto: set-in note, plates' provenance (atlas + Natural Earth PD base), and the working record with the attribution ledger named — "it is the book's thesis."

**Beyond the forks:** a Contents page (yes — 22 titled chapters, and The Caine Mutiny carries one); the **teleprinter treatment** as the one moderate-cost item — set-off message blocks drop to `\small`, slight letterspacing, ragged right, hyphenation off, so they read "as a different paper stock" (needs a markup pass across ~10 chapters; inline caps runs stay untouched; it explicitly rejects monospace); free `microtype` protrusion; and a mandatory post-reflow proof pass.

**Genuine taste calls it left to you:**
- **Ch. 7 letter** — defensible as-is (quotation-as-memory, "he had it by heart"); the alternative sets it as a document (block-indented, quotes dropped, roman — it specifically warns against italic).
- **Chapter numeral** — the two-deck recommendation vs. ratifying `N — TITLE` as a ledger device.

Its do-not-touch list matches ours: face, measure, asterisms (including top-of-page ones — standard practice), the raggedbottom price, and the disclosure's *text*, which it rates above WB's bar.

My read: I'd take the whole package as ruled, including the two-deck — the filename argument is right, and the ledger flavor lives in the book's documents, not its furniture. The only place I'd hold is the ch. 7 letter: I'd keep it as quotation-as-memory, since it's the one document in the book that lives in a person rather than a file. Sequence if you approve: margins/openright first (everything reflows), then front matter + TOC + colophon, then heads/two-deck with the 19→22 renumbering, then the teleprinter markup pass, then the proof pass. Your picks?

## ASSISTANT

Agreed on the diagnosis — zathura is doing first-page-alone pairing (rectos right), Chrome just tiles 1–2, 3–4 with no offset and no way to fix it from the file's side. One thing was worth doing on ours: the trade PDF now declares `/PageLayout /TwoPageRight` in its catalog, so viewers that honor the author's intent (Acrobat, evince, okular; zathura already behaves) open in correct spreads by default. Chrome ignores the key, but that's now provably Chrome's weakness, not an undeclared intent on our side.

One wrinkle worth the commit message it got: the obvious `\hypersetup` approach failed twice — pandoc's template loads hyperref *after* header-includes (so the command doesn't exist yet at header time), and deferring to begin-document runs after hyperref has already written its catalog special. Passing it as a load-time package option via `\PassOptionsToPackage` is the working pattern, now noted in the header comment for the next reader. Verified in the decompressed catalog: present in the trade build, correctly absent from the one-sided screen build. Committed and pushed (70e362e).

Cool. The mission-asymmetry item. IIRC, this is that "mission" mostly belongs to red, which is still true even if we don't have title resonance?

More broadly, we have the question of who owns / caused / drives / is responsible for / is agentic wrt the war.

Within the text, I think the answer is effectively treated as out-of-scope.

But perhaps we think it through for ourselves.

I don't think anything in the narrative aligns with a RSR-style rational-given-concealed-information-and-optimistic-projections resource grab. It seems more to me like a red misreading played out beyond the point of easy undoing, which blue may or may not have allowed to precipitate?

Thoughts?

---

## ASSISTANT

On the mission-asymmetry item: yes, that's the item, and it's not only still true without the title resonance — it got cleaner. With *The Mission* off the cover, the word is now almost purely red-register in the text. Red's people say it: Rylski's "out of respect for the mission," Zawadzki's journal formula "the conventional means adequate to the mission," "no amendment to the mission" in the enemy's silence-from-theater. And the one blue occurrence is the exception that ratifies the rule: the ch. 16 raid — the battalion commander "told the truth about his mission at his own insistence" — which is precisely the one blue operation conducted in red's grammar, a directed performance justified by its approval rather than its product. Blue otherwise doesn't have missions; blue spends the whole book trying to derive what it is *for*. A mission is a purpose fixed at approval time; the book's blue arc is purpose continuously re-derived under audit. Meanwhile the new title is the *other* half of the same joke: "The Baltic Approaches" is blue's founding-directive slogan, the one Rahn dismantles in chapter 1 ("It is a fine sentence and it belongs to the fleet"). So the cover now carries blue's inherited, unexamined answer, and red's word stays inside, in red's mouths. I'd call the item ratified on those grounds.

On the larger question — I agree with your read, and I think the text supports a more specific version of it than "misreading" alone. Assembled from what's actually on the page:

**Not a resource grab, a window.** Nothing in the text gives red an acquisitive motive, and the operation's stated object — "the port, and the canal behind the port, the Atlantic door the operation was approved to open" — is positional and instrumental, a door for a fleet in a wider war, not a prize. What the text *does* give red, three times over, is December: the sea closes in December, the cease-fire comes in December, and — planted in chapter 2 with the period furniture — "the missile deployments were due in December." That last one is the quiet load-bearer. A red leadership that believes the strategic board changes irreversibly when the Euromissiles land has a closing window in exactly November 1983, which is of course the month we pinned, and the month history itself filled with RYaN and the war scare. The book never says it, but every period detail points there and nothing points anywhere else. The approval argument Zawadzki witnessed — "over before it became the other kind of war" — is preventive-window logic, not RSR's rational-grab-under-concealed-information.

**"Beyond the point of easy undoing" is, I'd argue, the book's actual thesis about red.** The mechanism is on the page repeatedly: the norms as "the rent the operation owed its own approval"; the schedule "built for approval, in peacetime, by men who needed it to say what it says"; the relief directive that cannot contain the word relief because the grouping cannot be encircled, only "temporarily separated." Red's war continues because its bureaucracy cannot draft the sentence that ends it — someone must certify that conventional means have culminated, and the incentive structure (Kuragin's dates, Zawadzki's relief-drafted-politely) punishes exactly that signature. In the end the weather signs it. If you scale Hvidt's chapter-2 principle up to the top of the war: *when a plan fails it is usually at an orphan* — the war itself is the biggest orphaned number in the book. A misreading nobody owned, adopted by an approval chain, inherited by everybody, audited by events.

**Blue's contribution — "may or may not have allowed to precipitate" — the text treats as structural rather than decisional, and I think that's right.** The sharpest line for it is also in chapter 2: the alliance's alert catalog, "every numbered measure therefore a signal the moment it was invoked, with the enemy as the catalog's first reader." Blue's readiness grammar is legible to red by design, and red's reading of it is beyond blue's control. Add Kjeldsen's "I have seen an alert measure invented out of three coincidences before. By us, about them," and Aakjær's "almost nothing would look different if it were aimed at us — that is the property of the period we are in," and the book has quietly stated the full mirror-imaging epistemics: both sides plan against the other's best year, both read behavior, and in that season the signals are undecidable. Blue-as-corps did the opposite of precipitating — the quiet list is a study in muting. But blue-as-alliance spent its autumn emitting exactly the signal-shaped things (exercise season, the December deployments themselves) a fear-driven watch would misread, and the capitals that later spend a battalion for headlines are recognizably the same capitals that spent signals for deterrence in October. Blue could mute; it could not be silent.

**And the out-of-scope treatment is principled, not evasive.** Nobody at Rendsburg knows why the war happened, so under the book's own epistemic discipline nobody may say. The only causation testimony in the book belongs to red — "I sat in the room" — and even that describes an *approval*, not a decision: a room adopting an argument. Which is consistent with the after-action assessment's deepest claim: right is not a category the book can certify about anyone, including about the war itself. The instruments price the war's costs to the decimal; its cause sits permanently in the unpriceable column — "the operational initiative is no longer being exercised by either side. It is lyinging on the table" is the war as ownerless object, stated as early as chapter 12. The book's position, if forced into a sentence: *responsibility for the war's existence is unknowable from inside it; responsibility for its price is exactly knowable, and that is the subject.*

So my answer to your framing: yes — a red misreading of a closing window, ratified by an approval machine that could not reverse itself, against a blue whose contribution was the ambient legibility of its own deterrent, not a decision. Nothing in the prose needs to change for this; the reading is fully assembled from planted material, which is where it should live.

If you're aligned, two cheap dispositions: I mark the mission-asymmetry item **ratified** in the title brief with the sharpened evidence (the raid as blue's one "mission"), and I bank this etiology position as a short notes file — authorial background, explicitly not canon-for-insertion — so the final successor and any future reader of the record have it. Say the word and I'll write both.
